User clarification considered on 2026-10-12: a Cat-valued codomain does not
make all displayed transformations strictly natural. Over a walking arrow,
take constant families at Terminal and B. A lax displayed map can choose
b0 and b1 in B with a comparison b0->b1. Its two component functors are
strict; strict base naturality would exclude the example when b0 and b1
differ. This is a conventional normal-lax example in ordinary categories;
it is not proof that the raw prototype with its generic source-action
normalization faithfully admits that example. A deliberately
stricter displayed-map classifier is a possible separate design choice, but
is not selected for this migration. The existing Functord object projection
reduces to Transf(K,Cat,E,D), so restricting a rule's spelling to Functord
would not create a sound independent strictness boundary. Keep canonical
constructor-specific strict computation and the explicit strict-transfor
classifier separate from generic displayed FF.

default and implements the selected corrected-duality/profunctor Homd design
together. Retain lax/non-strict transfors and the native blended F/FF owners.
Keep the current lax machinery as the implementation basis for the explicit
weaker interface; this does not require strict wrappers or a duplicated
strict API throughout the new core. Do not replace native laxity with a new
encoding or independently supplied compositors.

tree. Preserve the paused application checkpoint separately.

## Preserve the selected profunctor design

Write T for dimension-one transpose, R for reversal from dimension two
upwards, and O for total reversal. C2 reverses dimension two alone. These
are the current design meanings, not authorization to equate distinct
higher actions. Primitive/derived names can be settled with the final owner
signatures before their consumers are migrated.

Source-preserving naming decision for the current migration: the actual main
baseline has `Hom_cat(Op_cat A,x,y) = Hom_cat(A,y,x)`. Its stable `Op_cat`
head therefore implements T (dimension one), not O (total reversal). Keep
that existing local behavior/name in this pass; do not silently reinterpret
its many lower-level consumers as total reversal or rename them mechanically.
Total reversal and R remain distinct operations when required. This corrects
the historical notation ambiguity without resuming the retired strict-v1
attempt.

For the Cartesian coefficient universe, this gives the required head type
`op : Functor (CoAt2_cat Cat_cat) Cat_cat`. Cartesian n-transformation
components are n-cells of the target: transposing their target reverses the
first transformation direction, hence Cat's dimension two. The native
`CoAt2_cat` projection is `Hom(CoAt2_cat K,x,y)=Op_cat(Hom(K,x,y))`.
The intended whole fibre transpose of variable `E:K->Cat` is the actual
composite `op o CoAt2_func(E):CoAt2_cat(K)->Cat`, matching the T_fib/C2
profile in the selected design below. This head/body candidate checks in the
prefixes recorded below; its remaining family consumers are not migrated. It does not claim
that pointwise transpose preserves an arbitrary lax transformation's profile.

Keep Cat-valued strict families and the selected coefficient convention:

```text
Hom_Q(A,B) = [A,[T(B),Cat]]

iota(F:A->B) : A -/-> B
  iota(F)[a][b] = Hom_B(b,F(a))

kappa(F:A->B) : B -/-> A
  kappa(F)[b][a] = Hom_B(F(a),b).
```

Existing Conjoint_prof and Companion_prof supply the opposite-endpoint
views under the current native naming convention. Retain those owner
relationships; no independent supplied adjunction is an input.

For strict E:K->Cat_adm and I_x=Hom_K(x,-), the selected whole section is

```text
S_E(x) = Transf_lax_{K,Q}(iota E, iota I_x).
```

Its y-component is E[y]->[T(Hom_K(x,y)),Cat]. The whole target uses the
covariant represented-index package Gamma_K:C2(K)->D_K with
Gamma_K[p][y]=kappa(f |-> f p), where D_K is the admitted diagram category.
Do not confuse it with the different iota-precomposition package over T(K).

```text
Homd_target(E) = Hom_D_K(iota E,-) o Gamma_K : C2(K)->Cat.
T_fib(E) : C2(K)->Cat,    T_fib(E)[x]=T(E[x]).
```

This is the design to IMPLEMENT and QUALIFY, not a prompt to restart the
ordinary-diagonal, primitive-Pi, combined-context, Kan/adjunction or general
ProfFamily/SigmaProf searches. Response 0099 itself retained the whole omega
coefficient, represented lift and native coherence qualifications. They are
implementation obligations, not grounds to claim that this design has
already been typechecked or fully proved.

## Structural whole operations and the meaning of lift

User clarification on 2026-10-13: a whole primitive with its correctly typed
component and higher-action projections is an accepted native implementation
method. Here "represented lift" names the operation taking a classified
family transformation U:E⇒D to a Q-valued transformation ιD⇒ιE, with
y-component κ(U[y]). The prototype already declares `Prof_cov_con_lift`
for that operation. It is not an instruction to construct an external
encoding or to replace the native projection ladder with coend calculations.

Qualify its actual type/profile, projection-order interactions, inherited
identity/composition laws and the Homd consumers that use it. A declared
structural contract must remain labelled as such; passing its declarations
alone does not qualify its consumers. Conversely, deriving the same whole
operation from the legacy tensor/coend calculus is optional comparison work,
not an automatic extra acceptance condition. Earlier chronological "next
required" lists that demand that derivation are superseded by this boundary.
Preserve existing derived owners and bodies where already applicable; this
clarification does not authorize postulating any failed equation.

The earlier interpretation of the ambient Homd root as automatically
carrying L⇒R is superseded by the intrinsic profile review below. Raw
transfors retain L⇒M and R⇒M; a conventional direction needs its actual
profile. Equality L=R, inverse laxity or a strict-transfor admission is not
required merely because E and its transports are strict functors. The
concrete consumers remain the existing native
`tapp1_func(homd_id_int(E))` action and
`fapp1_func(homd_id_src_func(E,x))` action, followed through their projection
ladders. General-FF normalization remains a separate composition diagnostic.

## Public defaults and removal of redundant strict wrappers

Reinterpret the public `Functor` object classifier as strict. Keep the names
Functor_cat, fapp0, fapp1_func, fapp1_fapp0 and the ordinary Hom/action owners
for that default. Do not create a parallel fapp0_sfunc/fapp1_sfunc story and
replace every ordinary type by an extra StrictFunctor wrapper.

The target naming contract is

```text
Functor(A,B)          strict default maps
fapp*                 their ordinary native action
hom_int(F), homd_int(FF)  retained blended public owners
LaxFunctor(...)       future weaker interface, reusing native implementation
```

The existing StrictFunctor, make_strict_functor, strict_functor and related
object-view operations become candidates for compatibility aliases/adapters
and then retirement. Migrate their consumers and confirm the required
computation before deleting them. Do not keep an obligatory strict_functor
wrapper as the permanent way to use the new default. Likewise audit the old
IsStrictFunctor properties as potentially derived/redundant on default maps.

This does not make all higher mapping categories identical. The current
StrictFunctor_cat has ambient transfors, whereas GrayHom_lax selects a
particular transfor profile. Only collapse category views whose entire Hom
profile agrees, not those that merely share strict functor objects. The
coefficient projection Hom_cat(Cat_cat,A,B) must have its declared full
profile; an extra object classifier named StrictFunctor is not a solution
to a difference in higher transformations.

## Preserve blended F and FF interfaces

Keep the public blended interfaces and projection ladder, including
hom_int(F), hom_con_int(F), homd_int(FF), homd_(FF,...) and the paired
covariant/contravariant action heads. The ordinary F is a default-strict
functor. Displayed FF is a transformation between family functors: its
components are strict functors, but its naturality need not be strict.

Reassessment prompted by the user on 2026-10-13: the previous plan and
cart_action prototype retained homd_int(FF) as an independent primitive. The selected
implementation direction is an identity-only Homd core and a defined relative
extension, while preserving the public blended spelling:

```text
homd_id_int(E)                 // primitive identity-only core
homd_int(FF : D => E)
  := Homd_target_map(FF) o homd_id_int(E).
```

This ownership is now implemented in the isolated homd_identity owner copy,
with its identity ladder checked; production integration remains open.
The identity core is not defined circularly through the general primitive.
The target action and this composite's type already check in the prototype.
User clarification on 2026-10-13: the complete foundational projection ladder
and its full required computation are acceptance criteria for homd_id_int(E).
Do not make a parallel complete general-FF reduction theory a prerequisite
for that identity milestone. The relative interface inherits the computation
of its actual composition; residual general-FF terms are allowed. Retain
existing public projection names as derived observations, without transferring
unconditional strict-naturality laws from the identity or strict-FF case.

Distinguish this implementation boundary from the mathematics: at fixed
x,u,y,v,f, the symbolic value `Hom(E[f](u),FF[y](v))` still depends only on a
strict component functor FF[y]. Laxness does not invalidate that formula;
it prevents erasing FF's base naturality cells. A probe for symbolic leaf
normalization is a separate composition diagnostic, not an instruction to
force general FF to compute like a strict transformation. Whole Q/lift and
generic intrinsic-action integration remain required by the overall goal;
the relative alias alone does not establish them.

This local factorization does not require unbundling ordinary hom_int(F),
making every Functord strict, or changing every public caller. It also does
not itself prove homd_id_int(E) is a strict transformation. The represented
lift, Q coefficient structure and identity-core whole x-action remain
prerequisites even without FF. The existing same-profile Op_funcd(FF) route
in generic intrinsic-action declarations must still be replaced or correctly
profiled; defining a relative alias alone does not qualify those declarations.

For the selected strict family profile the blended target is schematically

```text
FF : D => E
homd_int(FF)
  : Functord_C2(K)(T_fib(E), Homd_target(D))
homd_src_func(FF,x) : T(E[x]) -> S_D(x)
homd_src_sec(FF,x,u) : Obj(S_D(x))
homd_tgt_func(FF,x,u,y) : D[y] -> [T(Hom_K(x,y)),Cat]
homd_(FF,x,u,y,v) : T(Hom_K(x,y)) -> Cat
homd_(FF,x,u,y,v)[f] = Hom_E[y](E[f](u),FF_y(v)).
```

The target-y component is hom_con_int(F_xu,y) o FF_y, where
`F_xu,y(f)=E[f](u)`. This is compatible with keeping both blended owners.
The whole relative interpretation uses composition with iota(FF); it need
not introduce a second independent Homd theory or an operational comparison
parameter.

Distinguish FF's component functors from its displayed naturality: the
current Functord interface is a transfor classifier too. Strict defaults for
FF_y do not by themselves make the naturality cell lambda(q) an identity.
A selected fully strict displayed profile may have that computation; a lax
transfor profile must retain its original lambda. This affects which laws
are valid, not whether FF may remain a blended argument.

## Preserve the current native lax implementation

The existing `fapp*`, `tapp*`, `fdapp*` and `tdapp*` owners already retain
normal-lax action. In particular the nucleus defines

```text
fapp1_compositor(F,g,f)
  := tapp1_post_laxity_cell(id_transf(F),g,f)
```

through the whole internal action and displayed laxity extraction. The new
explicit LaxFunctor view must preserve that computation and higher action.
A defined accessor may remain, but there is no new primitive mu, independently
supplied compositor, or external record of equality proofs.

The first pass makes the current ordinary carrier/actions the strict public
default and preserves the native lax machinery as the basis for the explicit
weaker interface, reusing implementation wherever possible. The exact
compatibility/export boundary is selected by the owner audit; completing
the weak-category interface is not a prerequisite. It does not rederive
lax functors through Q_lax(A), nor require a new classifying-source theory.
The cited two-dimensional precedent for that alternative did not establish
an arbitrary omega implementation; no answer to that separate problem is
needed for this migration.

Keep the normal identity convention and each original comparator's direction.
Strict views may compute the appropriate intrinsic comparator to identity.
Lax views retain it. Do not install an unqualified lax-to-strict coercion.
Names and kernel views are to be selected in the first owner audit; a clone
of the semantic interface need not duplicate every downstream theorem and
normalization pipeline.

## Shared design only: lax functors between weak categories

The user's intended primary weaker interface is, schematically,

```text
LaxCat                          native weak/lax category profile
as_lax_cat : Cat -> LaxCat       strict-to-weak inclusion
LaxFunctor : LaxCat -> LaxCat -> Grpd.
```

Thus the current lax maps between strict A,B become the specialization
LaxFunctor(as_lax_cat A,as_lax_cat B). This is not a weaker interface forever
restricted to strict endpoints with weak objects bolted on later.

Surface elaboration may eventually omit the inclusion, but its kernel view
must preserve the actual structure and rule discriminators. There is no
reverse coercion sending an arbitrary weak category to the strict Cat
carrier. Otherwise strict composition/Hom rules would apply to it again.
The current strict-base lax implementation should be preserved so it can
serve this specialization; implementation of the full LaxCat carrier is
outside the first-pass prerequisite boundary.

The requested meaning of weakness is native:

- Associativity is not an unconditional unification equation in the weaker
  category profile.
- Its represented Hom/precomposition/postcomposition action is lax rather
  than silently strict.
- Higher comparison cells come from that same native internal action.

Do not substitute an operadic/computadic encoding or an external weak-category
framework for this design. Assess the available native operations and their
coherence directly.

There is real mathematical content to this proposal. If P_w(y)=Hom_C(w,y)
is lax, its intrinsic compositor for g:x->y and h:y->z has a component at
f:w->x with direction

```text
h o (g o f)  ==>  (h o g) o f.
```

The contravariant represented action has the complementary directional
bookkeeping. Their relationship, units and higher compatibility must be
checked in the same internal calculus. Two arrows in opposite directions
are not automatically inverse data. If the intended profile only retains
directed structural comparisons, do not silently describe them as invertible
associators; conversely, identify actual inverse/coherence requirements if
those are desired.

Removing one associativity unifier is not the complete implementation:
strict identity-Hom accumulation and other composition/naturality owners can
also impose associativity. For example, strict postcomposition accumulation,
evaluated at f, compares precisely the two bracketings above. Audit every
such route when the weak layer is eventually implemented. The current lax
functor implementation was built with strict category endpoints, so its
extension to weak endpoints also needs this audit rather than a claim that
one deleted rule supplies all coherence.

This is a shared-design obligation, not authorization to start that extension
before the combined strict-default/duality pass is complete.

## Useful sharing versus unwanted duplication

A native weaker-action layer can serve both:

1. lax maps between weak categories, including the strict-base specialization;
2. lax represented Hom action of the weak categories themselves.

The strict layer supplies stronger computational laws and canonical views
for the selected strict objects/maps. Shared intrinsic action, projection
ladders, comparator extraction, composition mechanisms and duality bookkeeping
are therefore useful common infrastructure. Separate classifier/action heads
and laws are justified where their contracts differ. Do not merge distinct
profiles through an object-only unifier merely to reduce the line count.

Retain non-strict transfors in the strict core. The category of categories
and its mapping profiles still need honest whole interchange/variance types;
strict functors alone do not turn every lax-transfor universe into an ordinary
strict Cartesian coefficient. Review this in the native interface rather
than introducing a general weak-category implementation as a workaround.

## One coordinated migration, bounded checkpoints

Prefer one coordinated target and one traversal of each affected owner to
two complete repository-wide migrations. An owner should be adapted to its
final strict/lax map profile AND corrected duality signature before migrating
its dependents. This does not mean one enormous unreviewed patch.

The previous action-profile integration deliberately changed both functor
composition and transfor naturality. Under the user's new default choice,
restore the appropriate strict-F preservation/accumulation laws while
retaining the transfor-laxity changes. Use the original owner diffs as a
source, not a blanket Git revert of that integration: it also contains
normalization, profiling, resource and consumer fixes worth preserving.

| Stage | Required first-pass outcome |
| --- | --- |
| SF-0 | Final strict/lax carrier and coefficient signatures; selected response-0099 Q design; blended F/FF retained; weaker shared-design boundary recorded. Inventory the previous migration by semantic owner and choose the actual source/version/qualification boundary. |
| SF-1 | Reinterpret Functor/fapp* as the strict default; migrate and retire redundant strict wrappers where valid; retain lax/oplax transfors and preserve native lax action/extraction for its explicit interface. Break the late-property/bootstrap dependency without a parallel strict API or supplied-compositor calculus. |
| SF-2 | Correct native dualities and their whole action, ordinary Hom, coefficient and represented-index owners. Preserve paired heads and arbitrary directed higher data. Qualify the core before a broad owner sweep. |
| SF-3 | Implement the chosen profunctor-based blended Homd ladder, including the identity specialization, whole x/u and y/v/f projections, and compatible strict-family Sigma/Pi. Correct constant-family Sigma-Hom at whole and higher levels. |
| SF-4 | Migrate the declared foundational ownership/dependency scope to the combined final signatures, including ordinary Hom/precomposition laws and retained explicit lax consumers. Do not perform a second full duality migration afterward. |
| SF-CLOSE | Exact semantic/computational contract, preserved intrinsic laxity, targeted diagnostic repair, completed required validation, and a handoff for separate downstream migration. Weak-category implementation and application equivalence tasks remain outside this first pass. |

Acceptance is foundational: strict identity/composition and higher action;
retained nonidentity lax-transfor cells and recoverable native lax-functor
action/extraction for the future shared-design profile; whole projection and
normalization order; correct Hom variance and duality action; actual native
Homd/Sigma comparisons. Reject the recorded wrong reverse-arrow derivations
for the intended type/variance reason. No theorem about global consistency
of Cat:Cat is claimed from these local repairs.

Existing affected application checks may run as integration gates. This does
not add application reconstruction or equivalence development to the goal.
Do not label the whole repository migrated while excluding broken dependents:
SF-0 must state the foundational qualification boundary and any separately
versioned or explicitly unmigrated interfaces.

staged-diff review. Preserve other branches/worktrees. No pushing, merging,
publication, PRs, history rewriting or cleanup is included. The first pass
uses this dedicated worktree; do not recreate or reset it on continuation.

### Next candidate: total opposite in negative sections

The bad input in the finite Hom_catd control used T on the fibre but C2 on
the base. It does not refute the analogous use of TOTAL fibre opposite.
Write R for dimensions 2 and above and O=R T for total reversal. The next
candidate, not yet implemented or qualified, is

```text
TotalOp_catd(E) = total_op o R_func(E) : R(K) -> Cat
total_op : R(Cat) -> Cat,       total_op(C) = O(C)

Hom_catd(E, X, Y) : Catd(K)
  X : Obj(Pi_cat(TotalOp_catd(E)))
  Y : Obj(Pi_cat(E)).
```

For constant E at C, X is a whole functor R(K)->O(C). Applying O gives
T(K)->C, since O R=T and O O=id. For constant E at Cat, the same calculation
produces the exact negative input of the checked `Functor_catd`. Thus the
constant-Cat specialization could retain its current outline, using total
functor duality instead of dimension-one transpose. This is a concrete
candidate to check, not a new primitive Pi or a claim that its full dependent
action has been implemented.

Prefer testing the previously discussed T/R primitive choice here: retain
the existing `Op_cat` as T, introduce the needed native R owner, and make
total O a named derived operation. Keep the already required C2 owner for
dimension-one fibre transpose. Their commutation/involution and whole
functor actions need native controls before promotion. Total fibre opposite
is also needed for the corrected negative Sigma total, so this is a shared
duality prerequisite, not a parallel Sigma theory. The selected Q/Homd
implementation remains required; this candidate does not replace that goal.

while the K-diagram transformation category remains the ambient non-strict
one. Its represented maps are to be defined from ordinary curried Unit Hom
and existing Cartesian whiskering. A whole contravariant diagram lift then
feeds the represented x-package; Homd keeps its existing blended FF input.

The initial test is the full root/source/y/v/f type and direct component
ladder, not merely a fixed-y body. Rejection conditions include a source-base
identification C2(K)=K, strict naturality forced onto arbitrary FF, reversed
noninvertible cells, or operational equality inputs. The coefficient and
contravariant diagram lift are explicitly new structural contracts in this
first experiment: their typing cannot be called a derivation from the old
opaque Prof_tensor or a qualification of its units/interchange. Further
native action and semantic qualification remain required before promotion.
The old tensor has only one-way co-Yoneda maps; the ordinary bicategorical
companion/conjoint source does not by itself prove the omega coefficient.

The experiment now has checked declarations and direct projection consumers.
In the emitted source, Q=`Prof_cov_universe`, D_K=`Functor_cat(K,Q)` and
Cart=`Functor_cart_cat`. Its coefficient Hom is

```text
Hom_Q(A,B) = Cart(A,Cart(T(B),Cat)).
S_D(x) = Transf(K,Q,iota(D),iota(Hom_K(x,-))).
Homd_target(D) = hom_(Gamma_K,iota(D)) : Catd(C2(K)).

homd_int(FF:D=>E) : Functord_C2(K)(Op_catd(E),Homd_target(D))
homd_src_func(FF,x) : T(E[x]) -> S_D(x)
homd_src_sec(FF,x,u) : Obj(S_D(x))
homd_tgt_func(FF,x,u,y) : D[y] -> Cart(T(Hom_K(x,y)),Cat)
homd_(FF,x,u,y,v) : T(Hom_K(x,y)) -> Cat.
```

The whole-first x/u/y/v/f application computes to the existing leaf
Hom(E[y], E[f][u], FF[y][v]). The y-projection is ordinary component evaluation
in Q, directly yielding the v/f functor. It does not use a combined dependent
context, a curry step for Homd, a primitive Pi, or a terminal-component
wrapper. A nonidentity base 2-cell has the correct contravariant index action;
both whole-first and capped-first action reach the existing precomposition
owner. Gamma's arrow component computes to the companion of represented
precomposition, rather than the differently oriented conjoint.

The represented fixed-endpoint functions are derived from the existing Unit
Hom, Cartesian curry and Cartesian whiskering. Two measured partial-evaluation
joins are necessary: opposite involution erases the original negative Unit
pattern, and fixed precomposition retains a stable point head. Their canonical
rules return the existing hom_con owner, including identity and composite
input functors. They do not identify the two ordinary variance heads globally.

General FF also has a derived target-side operation:

```text
Homd_target_map(FF:D=>E) : Homd_target(E) => Homd_target(D).
```

It is represented precomposition by iota(FF), pulled back along Gamma. Its
whole type, x-component, identity and composition check. Consequently the
whole composite Homd_target_map(FF) o homd_int(id_E) has the required relative
root type without taking a fibrewise opposite of FF. Agreement of that
composite with the blended homd_int(FF) is still a coefficient-composition
obligation; no equality is postulated from the matching leaf values. The
target family uses native blended hom_ with Gamma as its functor argument,
avoiding the unstable reassociation of its raw composite spelling.

Two ordinary represented-precomposition joins restore identity and composition
after capping hom_int's action. Their actual consumer is Homd_target_map;
the no-join control fails its identity comparison. These are strict functor
action laws for the canonical Hom construction, not strict naturality rules
for arbitrary FF. The displayed Catd source is retained explicitly when
whiskering FF so its native identity/composition heads are visible.

## Represented-lift comparison and reuse of the closed profunctor core

For strict U:E=>D, the lift's q-comparison is
`iota(E[q]) o kappa(U[y]) => kappa(U[z]) o iota(D[q])`.
The right-hand composite is a two-ended represented Hom. The left-hand
composite needs a map out of tensor, so merely declaring its object values
or off-diagonal action would not construct that comparison.

Investigate reuse of the existing fixed-endpoint Prof evaluation/abstraction
core. Enriched Yoneda specializes the right implication of Unit_B by
Companion(F) to Conjoint(F); evaluation at its identity then gives the
canonical companion/conjoint counit. Move only the needed existing owners
before Homd in a copied source, retain their product presentation, use
Cartesian module transformations for the coefficient role, and connect to
Q through actual curry/uncurry functors. Do not equate product and curried
carriers by a unifier, supply an independent laxity cell, or introduce a
general adjunction prerequisite. The whole comparison and bridge, rather
than a matching fixed object formula, are the required consumers.

This slice now checks in `q_closed_migration.py`. It relocates the existing
product-prof classifiers, fixed-endpoint opposite, represented reindexing,
tensor/map and covariant implication/evaluation/abstraction owners before
Homd. The product presentation is retained. Prof_cat's vertical Hom now uses
the Cartesian module-map profile required by the coefficient; its functor
objects are unchanged. Raw Functord maps are not silently admitted to that
profile, and general displayed FF remains available in the Homd interface.
Later product-prof map consumers still require this profile migration.

The new represented specialization is

```text
Prof_imply_cov(Unit_B, Companion(F)) -> Conjoint(F).
Prof_represented_counit(F) = Prof_eval_cov_map(id_(Conjoint(F))).
```

The first clause chooses the represented normal form for the existing opaque
right implication. Its semantic basis is enriched Yoneda and its parameter
naturality, [Kelly, section 2.4, equations (2.31)--(2.33)](https://tac.mta.ca/tac/reprints/articles/10/tr10.pdf).
This is an explicit implementation choice, not a claim that the kernel has
derived a general Yoneda theorem or an omega-level coend model. The counit
itself is a definition through the existing evaluation operation. Abstraction
of that counit computes back to the represented identity. No independent
laxity witness or general Adjunction object is supplied.

The bridge to Q uses the existing Op_prof_func to swap the product endpoints,
followed by ordinary Cartesian curry; the reverse map uses uncurry and the
opposite profunctor operation. Both are actual whole functors. Whole unit,
conjoint and companion views now compute to their existing curried Q owners;
variable module maps are retained in the Cartesian Hom. These are whole
represented comparisons, not conclusions inferred from matching fibres.
The curried counit has Q's unit as target and a curried legacy tensor as source.

Prof_cov_tensor_view_func is a whole tensor operation read through that bridge.
Prof_cov_composition_func is the actual proposed composition body on arbitrary
curried modules: uncurry both, swap, tensor, and curry. The counit also checks
against the whole tensor-view source. Its agreement with native Q composition
is still open. Three positive boundary controls currently fail:

- the general presentation round trip;
- comparison of Prof_cov_composition_func with comp_prod_func(Q); and
- typing the derived counit with native Q composition as its source.

The separate ordinary curry/uncurry control also fails to reduce a whole
arbitrary input functor on return. Existing old checks only observed the
expanded expression, so they did not establish that inverse law. The next
implementation must qualify the Cartesian closure's whole inverse computation
and higher action, then connect the tensor body to the Q composition owner.
Do not replace these obligations with an object-only decoder or a supplied
comparison relabelled as a derived inverse.

A broader ordinary-precomposition observation rule was explored and checked
with additional Unit/Hom joins. It is not selected: reusing Op_prof_func gave
the needed bridge without those new general rules. The removed candidate's
exact checked sources remain in the retained receipts. The selected source
keeps constructor-specific represented curry/opposite computations.

## Presentation review: shared product theory and curried coefficient view

User clarification on 2026-10-13: the earlier remark about using curried
profunctors throughout was a question/expectation to discuss, not a migration
instruction. The follow-up suggests that the curried type in the Homd core
can be a convenient nested-functor alias, without its own dedicated theory.
This agrees with the recommended minimal-change design. Correct the earlier
assistant attribution; do not treat the direct-curried experiment as an
accepted repo-wide replacement.

With Cart denoting the selected Cartesian mapping category, distinguish
presentation from endpoint convention:

```text
Existing Prof(A,B) objects are functors T(A) x B -> Cat.
Cartesian product-map profile: Cart(T(A) x B,Cat).
Its positive-argument-first curried category: Cart(B,Cart(T(A),Cat)).

Hom_Q(A,B) = Cart(A,Cart(T(B),Cat))
          = the curried Cartesian-map profile of existing Prof_cat(B,A).
```

The last line explains the endpoint swap in the existing
Prof_cov_curry_func(A,B) and Prof_cov_uncurry_func(A,B). No new primitive
curried-Prof classifier is needed: Q's existing Hom rule already returns
that nested mapping category. Its v/f evaluation uses ordinary application,
and the dependent y-component uses the existing transformation projection.
This is independent of a combined dependent (y,v,f) context or currying that
context; neither is introduced.

Keep the established product Prof owners for reindexing, tensor, implication,
shaped cells and their existing consumers. Obtain the curried coefficient
composition and units through the actual presentation functors, as in the
q_unit reference. Curried conveniences may have definition bodies through
ordinary application/whiskering or those maps when a consumer needs them;
do not introduce a second independently axiomatized profunctor calculus.
For the existing endpoint convention, reindexing reads

```text
Product: P[F,G](a',b') = P(F[a'],G[b']).
Curried: P_c[F,G][b'][a'] = P_c[G[b']][F[a']].
Q-Hom convention: H[F,G][a'][b'] = H[F[a']][G[b']].
```

The last formula changes the profunctor endpoint convention as well as the
order of arguments. Making the same public Prof(A,B) name use that convention
would force additional companion/conjoint, tensor-incidence and consumer
changes. Those changes are unnecessary for the selected Homd ladder.

Semantic qualification: Cartesian internal Hom of strict omega-categories
supports this product/curry comparison with strict higher transformations;
the ambient lax/oplax mapping categories have the distinct Gray closure.
[Ara--Guetta, paragraphs 18--21](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08832v1)
records that distinction. The comparison is not an unrestricted claim about
currying ambient lax transfors over products. Public FF remains a general
non-strict displayed transformation with default-strict functor components;
it is not silently restricted to the Cartesian coefficient's vertical maps.

Final consumer review confirms that representation and map profile must be
kept separate. The existing
[Hom-prof curry reviewer](../emdash2/examples/prof_curry_hom_views.lp)
already relates product Hom_prof/Unit_prof to curried hom_int using typed
proof-time comparisons. This is direct precedent for keeping both views,
not a requirement for a replacement profunctor theory. Meanwhile, the
[reindexing reviewer](../emdash2/examples/prof_reindex_components.lp) and
[shaped PathOut owner](../emdash2/emdash3_2_shaped_pathout.lp) use Prof maps or
shaped cells directly as ambient displayed transformations. Join mapping and
DefIso evaluation also rely on those existing projections. These are concrete
API dependencies; they do not by themselves prove that every such consumer
semantically requires non-strict naturality.

Therefore the q_unit prototype's Cartesian vertical Prof-map and shaped-cell
restriction is a coefficient experiment, not blanket permission to narrow all
public ProfMap/Prof_hom inputs. For the Q bridge, first use the already
available Cartesian product mapping category and its actual ambient
inclusion, sharing object carriers and operations. Qualify the required tensor,
unit/counit and shaped-action restrictions at their existing owners. Preserve
raw generic consumers unless a separately demonstrated mathematical
requirement necessitates changing their profile. Do not duplicate the entire
Prof theory or infer strictness merely from Cat-valued codomain. This profile
boundary remains an explicit promotion requirement of the selected design.

The final recommendation is thus: existing product Prof/public endpoint
conventions as the common theory; Q's nested Cartesian Hom type as a convenient
curried view; actual qualified presentation maps only where operations cross
that boundary; derived conveniences only when a concrete consumer needs them.
No new primitive curried-Prof classifier or mandatory migration of all
profunctor consumers follows from the Homd ladder.

## Alternative direct-curried experiment (retained, not selected)

After checkpoint 8d440ffb, curried_prof_migration.py explored an all-curried
owner copy from the pinned two-presentation reference. Following the user

declaration/reindexing/represented block rather than adding a second public
profunctor theory.
The legacy opposite/tensor/downstream suffix remains present as the next
migration boundary. The qualified prefix contains these actual definitions:

```text
Prof_cat(A,B) := Cart(A,Cart(T(B),Cat)).
Prof_reindex_func(F,G) := post(pre(T(G))) o pre(F).
Prof_reindex(P,F,G) := Prof_reindex_func(F,G)[P].
Prof_reindex_transf(r,F,G) := Prof_reindex_func(F,G)[r].
Unit_prof(A) := Rep_cart_func(T(A)).
Companion_prof(F:A->B) : Prof(B,A), value Hom_B(F[a],b).
Conjoint_prof(F:A->B) : Prof(A,B), value Hom_B(b,F[a]).
```

The existing generic product Hom is renamed Hom_prod_func in the upstream
copy. Its computations and the derived ordinary representation are preserved;
it is not a second Prof carrier. Reindexing has no product swap, curry,
uncurry or operational equality parameter. Unit uses the existing ordinary
representation, whose generic curry construction is still part of the
ordinary Hom foundation. The new Homd telescope is not obtained by currying
a combined dependent base.

The transparent Prof_cat alias is a measured implementation choice: a
candidate with only Obj/Hom projections left identity reindexing stuck at
fapp0(Prof_cat,Prof_cat,id_Cart,P). The alias shares native identity/composition
with its actual Cartesian category. This does not identify Cart with the
ambient mapping category or make all displayed FF strict.

## Op_funcd and Q profile clarification, 2026-10-14

The user's questions distinguish two separate choices. The prototype's
`Op_funcd` currently implements the strict-transfor action of Cartesian
family transpose; the legacy production declaration remains general. It is
not a decision that public FF must become strict. The first naturality
square reverses under fibrewise T. Moreover, T is not a standard Gray
monoidal/anti-monoidal duality, so an unqualified "replace lax by oplax"
omega-level operation is not yet justified. The
[intrinsic review](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_profile_review.md#why-the-checked-op_funcd-takes-a-strict-transfor)
records the exact interface and reference boundary. This qualification
constrains the next target repair; it does not add a new wrapper by fiat.

The two Cart levels in Q support the selected Cartesian product/curry
doctrine. The inner level constrains interchange within each module; the
outer level constrains maps between those modules. They do not make the
outer Homd y-section or public FF strict. The
[evaluation review](EMDASH_STRICT_FUNCTOR_EVALUATION_PROFILE_REVIEW.md#the-two-cartesian-levels-of-the-selected-q)
now records why a nested ambient-lax replacement is a different doctrine,
not a drop-in alias, and why the current choice is not a uniqueness theorem
for all possible Q designs. Keep this sufficient reference while qualifying
the actual required consumers; do not broaden its evidence to a claim of
completed coefficient coherence or use it to erase non-strict input action.

## Codomain-only transfor strictness reassessment, 2026-10-14

Reassessment prompted by the user's codomain-only strictness proposal on
2026-10-14: making every `Transf(K,Cat,E,D)` strict conflicts with the existing
constant-family section interface. For `E=const(1)` and `D=const(B)`, those
transfors represent ordinary functors K→B, and their intrinsic comparison
at p is the ordinary arrow F[p]. Global strictness would force equal endpoint
selectors and identity action along p. The walking-arrow identity functor
already refutes that combination. The
[intrinsic review](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_profile_review.md#reassessment-of-global-strictness-at-codomain-cat)
records the native owner links and the finite check (three ordinary functors,
two strict constant-family sections). No such global rule is selected or
installed. This is stronger than merely excluding an exotic higher example:
it changes the existing Pi/constant-family meaning. Named structural-head
computation remains available when its strictness is justified, without
making all Cat-valued transfors strict or requiring operational profiles.

## Section semantics and default reassessment, 2026-10-14

The reason previously given for using lax Cat-valued transformations in Pi
was preservation of the existing all-section meaning. It is not a theorem
that a foundational symbol named Pi must have that meaning.

For an ordinary base K and a strict category-valued E, the two contracts are:

```text
All sections:    s[x] in E[x], with arrows E[p](s[x]) -> s[y]
                satisfying the section unit/composition laws.
Strict sections: s[x] in E[x], with E[p](s[x]) = s[y]
                and identity comparison arrows.
```

With the corresponding strictly natural morphisms, strict sections form the
ordinary strict limit of E. All sections are functorial sections of its
Grothendieck total; they need not preserve the chosen opcartesian lifts.
These are distinct legitimate constructions. For ordinary K,B, all sections
of const(B) form Functor(K,B), whereas strict sections form the product of
one copy of B per connected component of K. In particular, for the walking
arrow, all sections form Arrow(B) and strict sections form B. The finite
check gives three versus two objects when B is also the walking arrow.
The complete higher-cell profile in the omega case still needs specification.

Thus a deliberately strict Pi can retain the first two old default-classifier
comparisons under the Cat-valued strictness exception and retire the general
Pi(const B)=Functor_cat(K,B) comparison. It need not introduce LaxFunctord
merely to preserve that old equation. A lax-Pi alternative preserves the
old all-section meaning and needs a genuine lax classifier if the default
Cat-valued Transf is strict. These are semantic choices, not notation changes.

Concrete consumers of the all-section meaning include constant-target
section postcomposition and ordinary functors viewed as sections. They must
be migrated or given a separate all-section interface if Pi becomes strict.
The selected Q-based Homd y-section has codomain Q, not Cat, so Cat-only
strictness does not by itself make that y-section strict. Its Cat-valued
outer root, and any conversion through ordinary Pi, are separate consumers
that must be checked. Neither the constant-section counterexample nor a
new Pi name alone settles the Homd construction.

Returning to lax ambient functors would retain more of the current evaluator,
composition and internal-action architecture: the evaluator's comparison
cells need not be equalities. It is an engineering alternative worth pursuing
as a comparison baseline, not a certification of the old duality, Sigma or
cartesian-closure claims. Strict functors with lax transfors remain a valid
mathematical doctrine but require the appropriate Cartesian/Gray closure
interfaces; the global-default migration exposed those additional owners.
No broad semantic switch is installed by this review.

For Homd distinguish family E:K->Cat, transport E[p], and displayed map
FF:D=>E. The forward leaf remains `Hom_E[y](E[f](u),v)` for the admitted
family doctrine. Composing its forward total arrows needs
rho(q,p):E[qp]=>E[q]E[p]. This can be a coherent nonidentity oplax comparison;
strict base composition is sufficient but not the only contemplated case.
A usual normal-lax comparison in the other direction instead supports the
reversed-filler formula `Hom_E[y](v,E[f](u))` at the ordinary local level.
Its full corrected omega telescope is a separate qualification, not an
automatic reinterpretation of the original one.

The older native-nerve branch's `lax_fibre_transport_associativity.py` and
`lax_compositor_associativity.py` were rerun read-only on 2026-10-14. The
first still finds one failed associativity triple among fourteen total
arrows with strict outer family composition and a non-strict transport.
The second finds one among eighteen with identity transports and a merely
lax-natural compositor. These are finite semantic checks of specified
composition formulas, not kernel consistency results or minimality theorems.
They show why changing ambient defaults alone does not admit every lax E.

Rewrite qualification must follow formation qualification. Local projection
beta need not be restricted wholesale to strict E/FF. Replacing E[q]E[p] by
E[qp] needs strict family composition; erasing a transport compositor needs
that transport's strict computation; erasing FF naturality needs strict FF
or a justified constructor instance. General comparisons remain extracted
native cells. Merely guarding the rewrite rules does not make an invalid
unrestricted whole Homd/Sigma declaration sound. If a family doctrine is
restricted, prefer one native family classifier or canonical construction
with its actual action, not operational equality-proof parameters.

## Cat-valued strictness and family admission, 2026-10-14

The two strictness requirements for E must be kept distinct:

```text
Strict outer E: E[qp] = E[q] o E[p].
Strict transport E[p]: E[p][ba] = E[p][b] o E[p][a].
```

If the ambient Functor objects remain lax, making only Cat-valued functors
strict gives the first equation, not the second. Likewise, making a
Cat-valued FF strict constrains its base naturality; it does not make each
component FF[x] a strict functor under lax defaults.

A restricted coefficient could separate these choices. Schematically it
would have strict functors and lax transfors as its Hom categories, and an
admitted family would be a strict map into that coefficient. This is not
mathematically the same as changing every ambient functor. The existing
`StrictFunctor_cat` is a full subcategory on strict functor objects with
ambient transformation homs, so there is a native starting classifier.
Neither that classifier nor a proposed `Cat_s` signature supplies the entire
coefficient universe's higher composition or the corrected Homd theorem.
Its Gray-style closure would need qualification, with no operational proof
payload in Homd.

The user explicitly excludes that alternative as the selected solution:
retain one universe, the public Hom/Functor identification, and admission of
every E:K->Cat. For an arbitrary F:A->B, the walking-arrow family with values
A,B and transport F already has strict outer composition (there is no pair
of nonidentity base arrows). Requiring every such transport to be strict
requires every F to be strict. Thus the global strict-functor choice is
forced by these combined architectural and Homd requirements, not by the
mere existence of a dependent Hom construction in mathematics. This
reaffirms the current goal direction without claiming its implementation
is complete or selecting strict transformations.

There is a second obstruction to treating Cat-valued strictness as an
isolated exception while retaining ambient lax F. For arbitrary F:A->B
and x:A, the existing blended represented family is

```text
H = hom_(F,F[x]) : A -> Cat
H[p](a) = F[p] o a.
```

If H is strict, evaluate H[qp]=H[q]oH[p] at id_F[x]. It gives
F[qp]=F[q]oF[p]. Unbundling the constructor does not avoid this: the composite
Hom_B(F[x],-) o F has the same action. Retaining genuinely lax F would require
a different admissible Hom/classifier contract. This argument forces the
composition equation; it does not by itself prove that every independently
retained compositor cell is an identity.

The finite Hom-detection check uses a normal-lax F:[2]->Y, where Y has one
object and hom poset 0<1 with composition max. F(01)=F(12)=0, F(02)=1,
with compositor 0->1. The two Cat-valued Hom actions are [0,1] and [1,1].
They differ at the identity 0. This requires no Op, Pi, Sigma or Q.

Cat-valued **transformation** strictness has a separate propagation issue.
The existing `tapp1_at_transf(epsilon,x)` internalizes an ordinary lax
epsilon as a Cat-valued transformation. Strictness there forces
G[g] o epsilon[f] = epsilon[gf]; the fixed-target mirror forces the
corresponding equality on the other side. These interfaces would erase
the ordinary lax naturality boundary. The finite mixed-family checker now
exhibits strict F,G and a normal-lax epsilon with two distinct sides of its
naturality square. Its prescribed Cat-valued internalization is not strict.
This example does not use Pi, so changing Pi's meaning alone cannot repair
the exception. A genuinely lax/mixed internal classifier could be a repair;
it is a further core interface change, not an installed solution.

The same square gives a conditional Homd check: a strict E may send a base
2-cell alpha:p=>q to a lax transformation E[alpha]. In a curried target
whose inner Cat-valued transformations have all been made strict, the
prescribed u-action needs a strict f-naturality square that this E[alpha]
does not supply. This rejects those combined projections and classifier,
not every corrected-duality/mixed Homd design. The old Cartesian-Q prototype
cannot therefore be treated as an automatically compatible donor for the
new Cat-valued exception.

Review evidence: both finite scripts pass with the new observations on
2026-10-14. These are semantic checks of specified contracts, not native
Lambdapi admission or a consistency certificate. The
[intrinsic review](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_profile_review.md)
records their scopes. No new LP probe or production rule was added for this
review. Preserve the uniform strict-functor migration, while separately
resolving the higher mapping-category/internalization and section contracts.

## Strict displayed maps without a Cat-valued exception, 2026-10-14

The user's narrower proposal is not equivalent to the blanket exception
provided the displayed and ordinary classifiers cease being identified.
Current source has all of the following connections:

```text
Catd_cat(K)                         ~ Functor_cat(K,Cat)
Hom_Catd(E,D)                       -> Functord_cat(E,D)
Functord_cat(E,D)                   ~ Transf_cat(K,Cat,E,D)
Obj(Functord_cat(E,D))              -> Obj(Transf_cat(K,Cat,E,D)).
```

Thus merely adding strict computations to the existing Functord spelling
would still affect every Cat-valued transfor. Removing only its direct
comparison with Transf is insufficient: the whole Catd/Functor-category
comparison and the object projection also expose the same identification.

The selective alternative keeps exactly the current family objects and
public Hom(Cat)=Functor equation, but changes the family-category arrows:

```text
Obj(Catd_cat(K)) = Functor(K,Cat)
Hom_Catd(E,D) = Functord_cat(E,D), with strict transfor objects

Catd_include(K) : Catd_cat(K) -> Functor_cat(K,Cat)
Catd_include(K)[E] = E
Catd_include(K)_1(E,D)
  : Functord_cat(E,D) -> Transf_cat(K,Cat,E,D).
```

These are schematic proposed types, not newly implemented declarations.
The inclusion has no generic inverse. Its hom action supplies the ambient
view of FF; no equality proof or separately supplied naturality witness is
an operational Homd input. This changes a category of families, not the
coefficient universe of their fibres, and does not reject any E:K->Cat.
The existing StrictFunctord classifier is historical admission machinery,
not proof that it already provides this whole category/inclusion.
Strict objects also do not determine whether every higher modification is
Cartesian; do not silently identify this proposal with a fully Cartesian
mapping category.

There are four concrete owner consequences:

1. The ordinary `tapp1_at_transf` and `tapp1_con_at_transf` remain in the
   ambient Cat-valued Transf. They do not gain strictness unless an invalid
   generic reverse inclusion is retained. Consequently the previous blanket
   strictness internalization counterexample does not reject this separated
   design.
2. To retain Pi's all-section meaning, its ordinary Transf comparison can
   remain, while its Functord comparison, object projection and Transfd
   projection must change. Pi(Const B)=Functor_cat(K,B) is then no longer
   contradicted by the strict-Functord choice itself. Alternatively Pi may
   use strict Functord and classify strict sections, with the different
   constant-family semantics recorded above. The full omega higher profile
   still needs qualification either way.
3. The prototype `homd_id_int` itself is typed as Functord. It must not become
   strictly natural merely because that name is reinterpreted. Either prove
   the selected root has that profile, or give the root an ambient Transf
   type while reserving strict Functord for the external displayed-map
   parameter. The old negative strict-admission assertion is a qualification
   boundary, not a mathematical impossibility proof for every corrected root.
4. The current `tapp1_post_laxity_transf` and mirror feed the ordinary
   internalizations into `functord_laxity_transf`, whose argument is
   Functord. Its native extractor in turn uses `fdapp1_int_transfd` and the
   Homd-dependent target. After the split that input cannot be silently
   admitted as strict. The actual whole extractor and required endpoints
   must accept ambient Cat-valued Transf. Changing just the terminal
   `tapp1_*_laxity` spelling or supplying a new standalone laxity witness
   would not preserve the requested native construction.

This candidate therefore simplifies an external strict FF without forcing
every ordinary lax transfor or every inner presheaf transformation strict.
It does not automatically settle the generic intrinsic-action obligation,
the identity root profile, or the independent joint Cartesian composition
obstruction. It is a localized classifier alternative worth assessing,
not an implemented solution or an instruction to narrow public FF already.
After the user noted that the necessary inclusion may make this an
undesirable design, the recommendation is to keep it outside the current
migration. Its mathematical plausibility does not make it a smaller repair:
the ordinary intrinsic extractor still requires the lax side, and the
existing family-category and section comparisons would all need adjustment.
Preserve those uniform comparisons while correcting their actual duality
and higher-action contracts.

The independent closure inspection found that the existing
`emdash3_2_gray_right_closure.lp` uses the admitted GrayHom_lax profile,

has no general parameter action/monoidal coherence in that slice. It
therefore cannot simply replace the nucleus's Cartesian comp_prod_func
as an already-qualified raw-transfor operation. Keep this source boundary
explicit during further implementation; no Gray or Cartesian universe
replacement was installed during this review.

This tranche is a source/dependency review. No new Lambdapi probe was run:
the changed conclusions follow from the declarations and actual extractor

## Lax Q, Gray uncurry and strict Sigma maps: consumer review, 2026-10-14

The latest user clarification selects both lax levels of

```text
Q(A,B) := Functor_cat(A,Functor_cat(T(B),Cat)).
```

Here Q(A,B) is the proposed Hom of the coefficient Q, not a new alias for
the existing product Prof with its current operations. The desired direct
v/f telescope does not need to uncurry merely to form this type or expose
its fixed-parameter projections. If uncurrying is needed, its target under
the repository's right-oriented convention is

```text
Functor_cat(GrayTensor_R(A,T(B)),Cat).
```

The Gray interchanger retains the directed comparison between mixed
factorizations. Cartesian uncurry would require those factorizations to
have equal images. Thus the closure objection is a reason to use Gray
uncurry, not a reason to make Q's transformations strict. Tensor order is
part of the contract. The repository already has gray_curry_R_func and
gray_uncurry_R_func in emdash3_2_gray_right_closure.lp, but their checked
source is the older classified GrayHom_lax slice with supplied whole inverse
data. Identifying its profiles with the new defaults and qualifying the
actual Q consumer remain work; no whole native equivalence is newly claimed.
The semantic closure distinction is supported by
[Ara--Guetta v1, paragraph 21](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08832v1#S2).

For FF:D=>E with strict transformation naturality, the proposed restricted
total map has the familiar formula

```text
Sigma(FF)[(x,u)] = (x,FF[x](u))
Sigma(FF)[(p,a)] = (p,FF[y][a])

E[p] o FF[x] = FF[y] o D[p].
```

The final equality expresses the strict input contract; it is not an
operational equality-proof parameter. It identifies the fibre-arrow source,
and naturality plus strict component functoriality identifies the two
composition formulas. This removes the exhibited lax-square obstruction.
Qualification of the chosen omega-level totals and all higher map action
is still required; the finite countermodel alone does not establish it.
An opaque strict-transfor carrier or justified named strict constructors
can express this local restriction without changing default Functord or Q.
No production signature has been changed by this review.

Further user question: should suitable FF strictness instead be baked into
the default input classifier? Distinguish full strictness of FF from the
smaller condition detected by the example. For conventional lax FF:D=>E,
write its already-extracted comparison as

```text
lambda_p : E[p] o FF[x] => FF[y] o D[p].
```

The prospective total-arrow formula is
`(p,a) |-> (p,FF[y][a] o lambda_p[u])`. Compatibility with vertical
composition requires lambda_p to be strictly natural in the fibre variable:

```text
FF[y][D[p][h]] o lambda_p[u]
  = lambda_p[u'] o E[p][FF[x][h]],       h:u->u'.
```

This allows nonidentity lambda_p and therefore does not require FF itself
to be strictly natural in the base. Full strict FF is a sufficient stronger
restriction, not the minimal conclusion of the finite counterexample.
The analogous higher compatibility and coherent base action still need
qualification. The selected ambient conventional-lax classifier does not
automatically supply this stricter fibre-naturality contract.

Semantic scope clarification, 2026-10-14: the claim that strict component
functors FF[x] are insufficient remains correct. They preserve fibre
composition, whereas strict naturality of lambda_p controls a different
mixed square. Neither implies the other by definition. Full strictness of
FF would additionally make lambda_p identity, which is stronger than the
condition detected here. For strict D,E over an ordinary base, a sufficient
map profile consists of strict component omega-functors, strictly natural
lambda_p at every fibre dimension, and the normal unit/composition laws

```text
lambda_id = id
lambda_qp[u] = lambda_q[D[p](u)] o E[q][lambda_p[u]].
```

These are semantic laws enforced by the intended internal interface, not
new supplied comparison/equality inputs. With them the displayed formula
for Sigma(FF) preserves composition. For a higher base, compatibility with
base higher cells must also be provided by the whole transformation profile;
the single displayed square is not an all-dimensional sufficiency theorem.

Formation of Sigma(E) is a separate question. For the forward arrow
`(p,a:E[p](u)->v)`, a normal oplax family can supply

```text
rho(q,p) : E[qp] => E[q] o E[p]
(q,b) o (p,a) = (qp, b o E[q][a] o rho(q,p)[u]).
```

Over an ordinary base, strict omega-category fibres, strict transport
functors, strictly natural rho at every fibre dimension, and the normal
oplax unit/associativity equations give a sufficient strict-total profile.
In particular outer E need not be strict or pseudo. A concrete ordinary
example has base [2], constant fibres (0->1), E[p]=E[q]=id, E[qp]=const(0),
and the nonidentity natural comparison const(0)=>id. Its total is an
ordinary category. Strict E is the special case rho=id. A normal-lax
compositor in the reverse direction cannot be inserted into this same
forward-filler composition formula without additional structure or a
changed contract. Arbitrary lax transports are a further independent
obstruction; reversing the outer compositor does not make them strict.

For a general omega-base, do not infer full strict-Sigma qualification from
strict E and strict E[p] alone while E[alpha] may still be lax. The known
all-dimensional Grothendieck reference uses strict diagrams valued in the
Cartesian coefficient; its extension to lax Gray coefficients has a Gray
total (Ara--Guetta v1, paragraphs 78 and 90). This is a comparison boundary,
not a selection of Cart for public Cat or Q. The goal's all-family admission
under the public lax-transfor universe remains a requirement to qualify,
not a theorem already established by strict default functor computation.
The distinction between outer-family strictness, strict transports and
higher fibre naturality must remain explicit in status claims.

Baking a sufficient condition into a native map classifier is compatible
with the no-operational-proofs SOP. It is different from accepting arbitrary
Functord and silently installing an extra equation on it. Making the latter
restriction the meaning of default Functord would require reassessing its
identification with Transf(K,Cat,E,D), the whole Catd mapping category, and
section/internalization consumers. It need not mean that Q or all ordinary
transfors become strict. This question is under assessment, not a decision
to restore the rejected blanket Cat-valued strictness exception. Review the
smaller comparison-cell condition before choosing a full-strict FF API.

| Sigma_func:Catd_cat(K)->Cat | Cannot retain this domain: its arrow action would still create a strict total map for arbitrary lax FF. Reprofile the whole operator's source maps, not public Catd or Functord. |
| sigma_map_transf and family-natural sigma_pullback_total_transf | Review endpoints and higher action together with the new Sigma_func domain. A restriction of one leaf is insufficient. |
| sigma_pullback_total_func(F,D) | Its direct input is a strict default functor F, not arbitrary FF. Its individual base-change construction can be retained subject to corrected-total qualification; whole family naturality is the preceding row. |
| hom_con_int_postcomp_func-based slice maps in emdash3_2_pullbacks.lp | Named represented maps are candidates for direct strict admission; qualify their whole action rather than requiring all transfors to be strict. |
| Arbitrary FF base-change/observation maps in emdash3_2_represented_comma_families.lp | Genuine contract change: restrict those strict-total outputs or replace them with an appropriately lax/Gray construction. Their current generality cannot follow from the restricted map. |
| section_total_mapped_func and section/total comparisons | Reassess the map/section contracts. An arbitrary lax section or FF cannot be presumed to yield the same strict total functor. |
| Native Homd intrinsic extraction | Preserve its general lax domain independently of totalization; correct its Homd/duality target. Do not restrict the extractor merely because sigma_map_func is restricted. |
| Presheaf/ring and homology projection consumers | Inventory the actual named map and ordinary/discrete guards. They are migration candidates, not evidence that every current use is strict. |

`S_E(x)=Transf_{K,Q}(iota E,iota Hom_K(x,-))`. The direct y/v/f ladder is
obtained by ordinary projections, with leaf Hom_E[y](E[f](u),v). No
combined context, curry substitute, primitive Pi, operational path or
supplied comparison cell appears. Its whole y-arrow action computes at
q:y->z, v:E[y], f:x->z to Hom_E[z](E[f](u),E[q](v)).

A structural fixed-x source functor T(E[x])->S_E(x) now projects to that
derived section and exposes its first-Hom action through ordinary lax y/v
transformations to precomposition by E[f](h). Source identity/composition
are checked before and after intermediate projections. The inner u/index
boundary equality stays negative; no strict naturality was imposed on
E[alpha]. The strict-FF relative section is actual composition with the
Q diagram map and computes to Hom_E[y](E[f](u),FF[y](v)). Identity FF
recovers the identity section. These results do not migrate public Functord.

Measured repairs were an explicit represented codomain guard, the stable
represented Q composition cut, a direct fixed-whiskering component and a
named diagram-value fold for the actual identity cut. The u diagnostic
needed canonical Hom_cat binders; that was not an operational change.
Intermediate transformation-composition rules needed annotated mapping
category guards for subject reduction.

Final evidence: 96 positive/33 negative assertions including the donor's
65/27; twelve adverse variants (eight assertion failures, two dependent SR
failures and two definition-formation failures). The final run uses 1.755s
and 171,336KiB maximum child RSS under 90s/2GiB, with SR enabled. Warnings
change 742->815, all 73 added bodies inventoried; complete symbolic joins
remain open. The LHS audit has zero unreviewed slots. Exact receipts,
source hashes and formation/dependency distinctions are in the
[verification](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/strict_homd_section_verification.json).

Both Q designs remain candidates. The literal Cart substitution fails at
the raw representation's inner target category, before testing a correctly
adapted Cart inclusion. The earlier finite noncommuting-square test still
records the stronger naturality required by Cart for the prescribed u
action. Neither this formation failure nor raw Q's passing typechecks is
a theorem about complete omega-level coefficient feasibility.

Next root candidate, not yet implemented: put the transpose on the
represented section target. With C=Functor_cat(K,Q) and a qualified
represented x-package Gamma:CoAt2(K)->C, define
`S'_E=Hom_{CoAt2(C)}(iota E,CoAt2(Gamma)(-)):K->Cat`.
Its fibre is T(S_E(x)), by the dimension shift in Hom. A candidate whole
root then has ambient type Transf_{K,Cat}(E,S'_E), with x-component
E[x]->T(S_E(x)). Transposing that individual strict functor recovers the
checked T(E[x])->S_E(x) presentation without requiring a same-profile
fibrewise T(E). CoAt2 here is justified by shifting dimension 2 of C to
dimension 1 of Hom_C; it is not a return to an unexplained Reverse12 base.
Test Gamma, the root's naturality and their projections before adopting
this presentation. Do not treat matching object fibres as a whole-root
construction or make the root strict merely because external FF is strict.

The outer homd_id_int/homd_int, full Q/mate coherence and intrinsic
extracted action, Sigma-Hom integration and production migration remain
required. The donor's known joint Cartesian contracts also remain outside
this slice's qualification. This is concrete progress toward the same goal,
not a narrowing of completion to a fixed-x or supplied-coefficient result.

## Coordinated whole Homd root and raw FF, 2026-10-14

The [implemented candidate](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_transposed_target_review.md)
closes the previously missing signed whole-root interface under the explicit
Q/mate contracts. It does not form the invalid same-default fibrewise T(E).
For I_x=Hom_K(x,-) and C=Functor_cat(K,Q), Gamma:CoAt2(K)->C has object
iota(I_x) and first Hom exactly the checked fixed-x source functor
`homd_id_src_func(I_x,x') : T(Hom_K(x,x'))->S_{I_x}(x')`.
Its two-cell direction is reversed and its next Hom retains the dimension-
three direction. No generic contravariant mate functor on raw modifications
is introduced.

The target is the actual whole definition
`S'_E=Hom_{CoAt2(C)}(iota E,CoAt2(Gamma)(-)):K->Cat`, with fibre T(S_E(x)).
The structural root has ambient type `Transf_{K,Cat}(E,S'_E)`. Its x-
component is E[x]->T(S_E(x)); transposing this individual strict functor
recovers the checked T(E[x])->S_E(x) view. The full identity-root
x/u/y/v/f projection computes to Hom_E[y](E[f](u),v). Objects of the
transposed section category are used without identifying its arrows with
those of the original section category. No primitive Pi, larger context,
curry substitute, operational path or supplied laxity cell appears.

The whole root pre/right action at p:x->x' sends u to the section at
(x',E[p](u)). The actual target-family action has the same object value on
the canonical section. This does not assert equality of its whole lax
boundaries. A nonidentity base two-cell alpha acts at u by the existing
source-u map for E[alpha][u], and a variable endomorphism is not collapsed.
Gamma's unit/composition checks use explicit structural mate laws and one
scoped proof-time Hom-index reading. A canonical Hom_cat diagnostic fixes
the bare-Hom alias inversion without changing operational action.

Only category-level CoAt2 and its ordinary strict-functor action are reused
from the earlier files. The Cartesian mapping-category duality declarations
are excluded. Whole Hom action under CoAt2 shifts to T on the Homs, and
the named Gamma reindexing exposes its actual image at the whole Hom owner.

The relative construction now accepts **raw ambient FF:D=>E**. Covariant
inclusion gives iota(FF), and ordinary precomposition in CoAt2(C) yields
a whole `S'_E=>S'_D` target action. Its composite with the identity root is
the raw-FF relative root. The experimental strict-FF names are wrappers
using the native strict-map inclusion; there is no second primitive theory.
After x/u, the raw root agrees proof-time with the actual section
composition `h_E(x,u) o iota(FF)`. That section view computes the leaf
Hom_E[y](E[f](u),FF[y](v)). The literal lower projection of the raw relative
root is not separately claimed to runtime-normalize to this leaf. Identity
strict FF does recover the identity section after root x/u projection.

Consequently, external FF strictness is no longer a prerequisite for this
root's *formation/compositional section construction*. This does not
settle its full intrinsic action or strict Sigma-map admission, and does
not select a new public Functord default. If public Catd/Functord is
narrowed, the current Hom_int-based target action needs its proper ambient
inclusion rather than the donor's automatic facade comparison. Preserve
the general ordinary lax internalizations needed by intrinsic extraction.

Final evidence: 122 positive/42 negative assertions including the 96/33
donor; thirteen adverse variants (ten assertion failures, two dependent
SR/variance failures, one formation failure). The untwisted target
reintroduces CoAt2(K)=K; Gamma over R(K) supplies O(Hom_K) where T(Hom_K)
is required. Subject reduction remains enabled; the final run uses 3.255s
and 180,992KiB maximum child RSS under 90s/2GiB. Warnings change 815->898,
all 83 added bodies inventoried; full symbolic joins remain open. The LHS
audit has zero unreviewed slots. Exact evidence is in the
[verification](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_transposed_target_verification.json).

Next: qualify the actual intrinsic comparison-cell extraction through
this root, including noninvertible action, and the Q/mate coherence needed
by that consumer. Retain both Q design comparisons, but do not undo the
new whole-family variance correction to resurrect the old T(E) source.
The root/type milestone now has a native candidate; this is not completion
of all coefficient semantics, Sigma-Hom, whole duality, public migration
or required downstream validation. The source donor retains known legacy
joint Cartesian contracts, and local negative controls do not certify
global consistency.

For the subsequent Sigma-Hom variance review, a dimensionally coordinated
candidate worth testing is a negative total for H:T(I)->Cat:
`Sigma_con(H)=TotalOp(Sigma(TotalOp_catd(H)))`.
The inner family's base is R(T(I))=O(I), and total O restores base I and
the original fibre objects. This is not an implemented Sigma-Hom theorem
or an admission result for arbitrary higher families; Sigma formation and
composition profiles remain a separate obligation. It uses the existing
total-duality construction rather than a duplicated Sigma theory.

## Intrinsic-action consumer of the new root, 2026-10-14

Checkpoint `56509f7b` retains the whole-root candidate. The next
[native intrinsic-action owner and projection definitions](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_projection.lpfragment)
adapt the existing internal-action architecture to its transposed target.
For FF,GG:E=>D, write B(FF,GG)=target_map(GG) o h_D o FF. The whole
internal action now has type
`Hom(FF,GG) -> Hom(B(FF,GG),h_E)`, reversing the old root-level direction.
After x/u projection, T(S_E(x)) reverses this arrow back to the intended
`h_E(x,u) -> h_D(x,FF[x]u) o iota(GG)` in S_E(x).

The y/v/p projections are definitions through native component evaluation.
Specializing the internal action to id_FF produces an actual Hom functor
`Hom_E[y](E[p]u,v) -> Hom_D[y](D[p](FF[x]u),FF[y]v)`.
Its object action transports a filler; its next Hom acts on a higher filler.
Applying it to id_(E[p]u) defines the forward laxity cell
`D[p](FF[x]u) -> FF[y](E[p]u)` without a supplied comparison or equality
payload. The relative section comparison needed for typing reuses the
preceding native proof-time composition reading.

This introduces one structural whole internal-action declaration, following
the old core owner, and no rewrite/unification rules. It is not a derivation
of that whole action's coherence from Q. The whole-u extracted transformation,
default identity/pre-leg normalization and compatibility with Q's mixed
higher action still require implementation/qualification. In particular,
the higher-filler typecheck does not establish all omega-level coherence.
No strict Sigma map of a lax FF, or production migration, is claimed.

The [focused replay](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_projection_replay.py)
checks this one candidate with a higher-filler consumer and two relevant
negatives (wrong root direction and noncommuting FF boundaries). No removal
matrix was added. The final run passes with SR enabled under 90s/2GiB;
the 898 critical-pair bodies are unchanged. Three additional diagnostics
come from those expected negative assertions. The LHS audit remains zero
unreviewed slots. [Exact evidence](../emdash2/audits/strict-default-action-boundary/homd_intrinsic_projection_verification.json)
records the authoring/source hashes, receipt, resource measurement and warning
comparison. Next qualify the whole-u comparison and the mixed higher action
required by this concrete consumer, rather than adding type-only root variants.

The subsequent mixed-three-cell review linked at the top of this plan now
identifies a failure of the unchanged standard-Gray higher profile. That
evidence supersedes proceeding directly to a whole-u extractor in this
same target. The user asked whether Cart would provide a quicker route:
nested Cart requires the noncommuting square of E[alpha] to commute even
when FF=id. A correspondingly stricter family input could remove that
obstruction, but is outside the retained all-family requirement and would
still need its own whole-action qualification. The stronger old public-Cart
universe prototype cannot be imported as qualification of Cart Q under
public Hom(Cat)=Functor_cat.

## Cartesian coefficient family consumer, 2026-10-14

The latest Cart-family implementation is an experimental comparison,
following the user-requested matrix, not a declaration that the overall
goal's public defaults or family classifier have migrated. Its exact source,

review. The early composition unit rule required explicit classifiers in

## Deferred improvements under the migration boundary

These are review opportunities, not prerequisites or selected changes:

- Replacing the existing strict-transfor carrier/view with aliases derived
  from a new mapping-category presentation solely to simplify ownership.
- Reorganizing every mapping category into a new uniform profile hierarchy,
  or changing the existing ambient transfor convention beyond what a
  demonstrated migration dependency requires.
- Turning derived whiskering conveniences into additional primitive
  interfaces merely because their old coefficient specialization no longer
  typechecks. First seek the corrected derivation at the existing owner.
- Factoring or splitting the nucleus and redesigning the action calculus
  while traversing it for this migration.

The existing shared-design-only weak-category/LaxFunctor work stays at that
boundary. None of these items replaces a required strict-default, duality,
Q/Homd, Sigma or validation outcome.
